## 1. Resume here

1. Work in **`D:\git\mgs-integral-native`**, even if the shell opens in `D:\git\MGSR`. Read [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md), this ledger's status and latest handoff, then the relevant HTML sections.
2. Inspect `git status --short`, branch, HEAD and the relevant diff before editing. The approved starting branch is `feature/godot-renderer-sdk`; the recorded HEAD is `ef747b1`. Preserve inherited changes, especially FSR2 and the user's renderer settings.
3. Read the active goal using the goal tool if available. Continue it; do not create a duplicate or interpret lost conversation context as a fresh project. If the tool is unavailable in a later session, this file still records the authorized objective.
4. Resume the interrupted task from Current handoff first. Only after completing it, select the next unchecked task whose prerequisites are satisfied. Mark it `IN PROGRESS` in the Current work table. Read its source owners before designing an adapter or public API.
5. Complete a bounded implementation slice and the checks relevant to it. Record changed paths, exact commands/results and evidence before checking the task off. A checked task means its stated deliverable exists and its checks passed.
6. Before a session ends or context is compacted, update Current work, the affected task IDs, evidence and the latest handoff. Leave the next concrete action and any running process/build directory ownership explicit.
7. User direction, 14 October: never capture screenshots, frame dumps or scene snapshots during real gameplay or performance measurements. Keep diagnostic capture explicitly opt-in and blocked by performance mode. Capture-enabled runs are visual evidence only. Do not start competing background game/GPU probes while the user tests; off-screen windows did not reliably prevent interference.

Do not redo passed historical work just because the context was compacted. Do not advance a milestone on the basis of code presence alone. Source recovery, compilation, automated behavior, measured performance and human visual/playability acceptance remain separate labels.

Latest steering (13 October): full Godot runtime must use the same map content/authoring as the editor. Provide a saved **Legacy through Godot** mode without scene lighting/PBR/mod additions, alongside preserved Legacy0.2. User clarified that the dark PBR capture was expected from the original point lights alone: raise ambient through an ordinary Environment in BOTH editor imports and runtime. Do not restore native vertex lighting merely to conceal that darkness. Investigate actual startup latency; do not attribute it to Debug without measurements.

- `mod_export.gd` now checks every collected file against prepared original source/file hashes, before text/binary resource processing. `export_provenance.gd` indexes both available prepared catalogs including UI dependencies and cinematic source/prepared data. Known original copies are rejected after renaming and the message identifies their source. No user ZIP/source asset is changed.
- `export_gltf.gd` inspects glTF and GLB JSON/binary chunks, external URL-encoded relative dependencies, base64 buffers/images and image buffer views, including slices within combined buffers. Bounds/type/base64/header errors produce actionable export errors. Exact original buffer/image payloads are rejected; custom external, embedded and binary models remain supported. A custom exported glTF is extracted and loaded through GLTFDocument in the test. This is not a complete glTF schema validator or a semantic detector for arbitrarily re-encoded originals.

**Completed M5.04a API implementation retained for custom mods:**
- Native include/src `mgs_inventory_actions` and `mgs_godot_inventory_actions`, vendor `menu/inventory_actions.h` plus item.c/weapon.c/menuman.c; Godot inventory_actions/button/controls, runtime/API/session wiring. - Protocol15.30 op31:652-byte view/ack,32-byte command, optional bit2147483648u, selected0xffffffff, required0x1fffffff. Signed target-1 means None, sides items0/weapons1. ## 2. Reachable objective and completion boundary

Deliver a **complete Godot presentation layer and practical modding SDK** for the configured Integral main campaign on both discs. Keep native simulation authoritative. One launcher selects Legacy 0.2, Godot, or an installed compatible community renderer. Modders can edit original-content references, add or replace visuals, attach them to dynamic native actors, change UI and camera presentation, test in the native game, and export/reopen packages without shipping original assets.

The first supported delivery is the current Windows native x86 / Godot x64 pair. Keep interfaces portable and input in SDL. A relocatable player package, editor SDK and one asset-free external renderer example prove the distribution and extension contracts. Supporting arbitrary third-party renderers, every GPU or a new gameplay engine is not required to finish this goal.

### Confirmed decisions

- Native code owns gameplay, collision, script execution, animation evaluation, audio, saves and simulation timing **for now**. Godot owns presentation and sends explicit validated actions to native providers.
- The final Godot image uses **no legacy primitive/VRAM raster rendering**, including UI, particles and screen effects. Locally imported original artwork is allowed; original art and the old renderer are different things.
- The default UI preserves the original layout using editable **Godot Controls and Themes**. Individual elements and the complete UI can be replaced. In-game settings must change real settings, not decorative menu values.
- Save/load uses a flat PC save list and New Save, preserving the original MGS fonts, green-on-black colors and framing. Native memory-card storage remains underneath; no card/block selection is presented.
- Use ordinary Godot nodes, the Scene tree, Inspector, materials, animation tracks and signals. Integration panels handle content, bindings, diagnostics, packages and native test sessions; they must not duplicate normal light or mesh creation tools.
- Map selection is a searchable list, not a dropdown. Show readable names with stage IDs, gameplay/cinematic classifications, disc availability and direct/inherited active-mod edits. Unprepared entries remain visible with reasons.
- Dynamic objects are first-class targets. The user clarified that the bars are the platform railing. Platform and railing need separately editable part targets sharing native body motion; other articulated actors still need independent native anchors. Parenting should handle motion; scripts or property bindings handle optional visual reactions.
- Map and asset authoring uses prepared disc data without a running game or F7 prerequisite. Runtime inspection supplements authoring rather than creating the only editable scene.
- Object hiding/replacement remains **visual only**. Native collision, boarding, movement and story logic stay intact.
- Cinematic-only maps and authored camera tracks are in scope. The first camera API preserves native clip duration, event/audio timing and gameplay ownership.
- Mods ship custom assets and sparse edits/references. Original meshes, textures, UI atlases, fonts, animations and movies must be excluded through a transitive dependency audit, not only directory names.
- Preserve Legacy 0.2 behavior as a selectable baseline. Remix integration is deferred. No JENOVA/full-RT work. Do not restore the rejected custom dock rebuild or puddle-reflection experiment.

### Limits that keep this deliverable finite

- Freeze the campaign/stage/variant inventory from actual configured content in M0. New discoveries inside that inventory remain required work; omissions cannot be renamed out of scope to pass.
- Renderer switching restarts the session. General rig retargeting, gameplay migration into Godot, arbitrary cinematic event retiming, live rewind and VR mission expansion are later work.
- First model replacements keep the established native rig contract. Static scenery, textures, materials and attachments remain independently editable.
- Frame pacing is tested on a declared reference preset and the available RX 9070 XT system at 2560Ã—1440. Record the actual viewport sizes, settings and mod hashes. Arbitrary user-authored scenes cannot have an unconditional 60 FPS guarantee.
- Preserve the native approximately 59.94 Hz cadence (about 16.683 ms) rather than changing simulation to force a rounded 60. Report percentiles and stalls; average FPS alone never passes acceptance.
- Human campaign review stays a separate pending label where it has not happened. Do not spend sessions tuning automated players or attempting automated encounter completion. Deliver the implementation and focused evidence for the user's playthrough.

### Existing capability is partial

- The renderer combines semantic world meshes with original command-based composition. UI and some effects still depend on the compatibility image path. Full Godot image ownership has not been achieved.
- [GODOT_PROTOCOL.md](GODOT_PROTOCOL.md) documents the present bridge. Stable asset IDs and runtime instance IDs are not yet a complete authored actor identity/lifetime contract.
- `visual_mod_api.gd` API v1 exposes stage, camera, instances, current native actor lookup and snapshot/state notifications, with visual GDExtension lifecycle hooks. `native_actor_binding.gd` now supplies a script-based dock binding with normal child parts; the HTML's additional named anchor/resource classes remain proposed APIs.
- Existing model replacement uses a strict rig/mesh-slot contract. Do not imply arbitrary character replacement or retargeting is supported.
- Existing ZIP import/export, model/material editing and map overlays are useful foundations. Editor map choices and stage aliases remain limited; current aliases include `d00a â†’ s00a` and `d01a â†’ s01a` with original-content matching. They are not a general cinematic scene resolver.
- The launcher currently infers a Godot build from compile-time `GodotRoot`. There is no general saved renderer-ID selection or public plugin ABI. [RENDERER_MODULES.md](RENDERER_MODULES.md) describes a source-level renderer boundary.
- Generic elevator source supports a body and optional secondary object in `vendor/mgs/source/takabe/elevator.c`. The actual dock declaration has no `-f` secondary model. [Native binding notes](GODOT_NATIVE_BINDING_NOTES.md) record its body, state/callback evidence and coordinate traps. The user identified the platform railing. Gameplay and both cinematic elevator tracks keep its five parts rigid together; do not invent secondary gate motion.

## 5. Acceptance and evidence rules

Each completed task entry needs its task ID, source revision/diff identity, relevant content/mod versions, changed paths, commands and results. Keep large logs/images under ignored `build/`; retain concise reproducible conclusions and paths here. If local artifacts disappear, label their evidence unavailable rather than quietly claiming a rerun.

| Acceptance case | Required evidence | Owning tasks |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Legacy/Godot/community selection | Same launcher, persisted renderer choice, shared saves, missing/incompatible package errors | M1.08â€“M1.12, M8.05â€“M8.07 |
| Dynamic elevator/bar replacement | Independent anchor motion, child offsets, state reaction, native callbacks/collision retained, ZIP round trip | M2 |
| Identity/lifetime | Identical assets, multiple spawns, generation reuse, conditional instances, reimport, reload and reconnect | M0.07, M1.03â€“M1.07, M2.08â€“M2.09 |
| Editor workflow | Fresh workspace open/edit/run/export/reopen, correct selected mod and scene | M3.03â€“M3.12 |
| Original-content exclusion | Transitive dependency inspection including embedded originals and local camera/UI resources | M3.11, M6.03, M8.08 |
| Mod layering | Deterministic priorities, exact scope, conflicts and missing dependencies shown to user | M3.05â€“M3.06 |
| Complete Godot image | All inventory categories tested with old raster path disabled; counters at zero; no hidden legacy framebuffer | M4.01, M4.13, M5.13, M7.07 |
| Original-style and custom UI | Real native actions/settings, persistence, supported navigation/localization, sharp UI under FSR | M5 |
| Camera/cinematic-only scene | Edit/export/reopen/native playback, off-axis visibility, timing and skip/end handoff | M6 |
| Native behavior preservation | Focused same-input state/callback checks where meaningful; actual human playability labeled separately | M2.10, M5.01, M6.06, M7.12 |
| Cadence | Complete measured frame stream for declared workloads, distributions and strict check result | M0.03, M7.08â€“M7.11 |
| Distribution | Clean-profile and relocated-folder checks with final artifact hashes | M8 |

### Performance gate

The current [performance runner](../tools/test-godot-performance.ps1) requires at least 600 measured frames after warm-up. It fails if **any** measured frame exceeds the native target by more than 2 ms, or if the mean exceeds the target by more than 50 microseconds. Keep these criteria unless a future user-approved requirement changes them; do not relax them to hide regressions.

Record competing GPU workloads: the user previously warned that another game could be running. A contaminated run is inconclusive evidence, not permission to close the gate. Do not stop unrelated applications. Keep screenshots/debug readbacks out of timed samples. Report loads/compilation separately with explicit boundaries; do not reclassify periodic in-game stalls as loading.

The existing runner covers a scoped gameplay case, not the whole campaign or a full cutscene. M0/M7 must add appropriate measured cases before claiming wider acceptance. FSR2 image quality, authored shadows and lighting remain part of correctness while optimizing.

### Commands and operational notes

Run commands from `D:\git\mgs-integral-native`; inspect script parameters after they change.

```powershell
Set-Location -LiteralPath 'D:\git\mgs-integral-native'
git status --short
git branch --show-current
git log -1 --oneline

# Start the SDK editor when interactive verification is needed.
& '.\.tools\godot-4.8-dev7\Godot_v4.8-dev7_win64.exe' --editor --path '.\renderers\godot\project'

# Broad renderer suite at an integration boundary, not after every documentation edit.
& .\tools\test-godot.ps1

# Strict reference gameplay measurement; preserve the input ZIP.
& .\tools\test-godot-performance.ps1 -ModZip '.\build\mod-exports\local-780062320.zip' -Resolution 2560x1440

# Scope scans to changed implementation files; act on all blocking findings.
aislop scan --changes --json
```

Use only one CMake/Ninja writer per build directory. Relevant native/platform changes require the appropriate CTest suites and a real-disc boot. Vendor modifications must be exported to `patches/` and verified through reproducible bootstrap application. Do not launch background helpers in visible windows. A source-only scene test cannot establish runtime transport or gameplay behavior.

`PROGRESS.md` contains historical mixed-encoding bytes and CRLF line endings. Preserve them with targeted byte-level edits if a UTF-8 edit tool refuses the file; do not re-encode the whole history. Use the repository's normal line-ending configuration for `git diff --check`.

## 6. Existing evidence: do not overclaim

| ID | Evidence | What it establishes / limitation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| E00 | [Native campaign progress](../PROGRESS.md), preserved 10 October recordings/checkpoints | User completed one native campaign route; not Godot campaign acceptance |
| E01 | `build/fsr2-check/suite.log`, `build/godot-tests/da0b5fedbe484cd2914cec40d49aafe6/` | Prior complete Godot CPU/GPU/protocol suite passed, including FSR coverage and resolution/HUD checks; evidence predates expanded implementation |
| E02 | `build/fsr2-check/aislop.json` | Prior FSR implementation scan: score 100, no findings; not a score for later changes |

## 7. Source map and reuse notes

| Area | Start reading here |
| --- | --- |
| Current protocol and renderer interface | [GODOT_PROTOCOL.md](GODOT_PROTOCOL.md), [RENDERER_MODULES.md](RENDERER_MODULES.md); trace the native producers named there |
| Godot world/runtime | `renderers/godot/project/runtime.gd`, `scene_view.gd`, `renderer.gd`, `world_blit.gd`, `depth_capture.gd` |
| Existing mod APIs and resolution | `renderers/godot/project/visual_mod_api.gd`, `mod_registry.gd`, `model_contract.gd`, [GODOT_MODDING.md](GODOT_MODDING.md) |
| Editor workflow | `renderers/godot/project/addons/mgs_editor/`, particularly map/model sessions and package actions |
| Launcher | Find `GodotSession`, `Settings` and compile-time `GodotRoot` references; replace inference with descriptors without conflating renderer config and common saves |
| Elevator | `vendor/mgs/source/takabe/elevator.c`; inspect `GM_ActObject2` body/secondary updates, visibility and native state/callback ownership, then trace the dock declaration |
| Validation | `renderers/godot/project/tests/`, `tools/test-godot.ps1`, `tools/test-godot-performance.ps1`, native test targets documented in build scripts |

Historical reuse is read-only in `D:\git\MGSR`. `archive/godot-20260924` was verified as a directory, not a Git ref. Read its files directly; do not check out or modify that project's dirty working tree.

- `scripts/DmoCameraTrack.cs`: editable camera eye/target/roll/zoom properties.
- `scripts/DmoTimeline.cs` and `src/content-compiler/DmoAnimationCompiler.cs`: native 30 Hz clip key timing and generated animation tracks.
- `scripts/StageCutsceneCameraPath.cs`: editor Path3D controls, clip IDs and source frame-number mapping.
- `scripts/StageElevatorActor.cs`: separate body/door presentation patterns; do not import its alternative gameplay implementation.
- `docs/SCENE_AUTHORING.md`: useful workflow history, but copying whole original scenes into a mod is not an acceptable export strategy.
- Current historical-project `docs/SCENE_TOOL_REUSE.md`: conditional GCX declarations and source provenance. Unknown transforms must remain unresolved; all script branches are not simultaneously active actors.

## 8. Decisions, investigations and deferred work

| Topic | Decision / next experiment |
| --- | --- |
| Native versus Godot gameplay | Native now. Reopening ownership is a future user decision, not an implementation shortcut. |
| Default UI | Original layout as editable Controls, with custom UI supported. No unresolved preference. |
| Exact public API names | Not frozen yet. M0/M1 produce source-backed contracts; HTML examples do not establish implemented APIs. |
| Elevator anchors/state | Trace source/declarations in M0.06; prove independently in M2 before broad adapter rollout. |
| UI draw/state coupling | Trace and test one HUD/inventory/settings slice before retiring all UI drawing. |
| Scene/clip aliasing | Replace limited aliases with explicit authored/content relationships; investigate within M0/M6. |
| Camera geometry coverage | M4.02 must remove old-camera submission limits before M6 edits can be accepted. |
| Original asset ownership | Dependency provenance survives save/reimport and embedded resources; exact packaging design in M0.10/M3.11. |
| Performance | Preserve FSR2 and authored features; measure recurring CPU/GPU work. Current measurements do not pass the final gate. |
| Human campaign review | User performs playthrough after implementation handoff. Record pending honestly and keep other authorized work moving. |
| RT, arbitrary retargeting, gameplay migration, live rewind | Deferred outside this goal. |
| Rejected dock rebuild and reflective puddles | Removed at the user's request. Do not restore. |

If an investigation reveals a genuine choice affecting user-visible behavior, ask a concise question while continuing independent tasks. Ordinary source tracing, reversible implementation and relevant checks are already authorized. An unanswered optional question is not permission for an irreversible action.

### Authored camera cuts and temporal history - M6.04a / M6.08b

- Previous reply answered the map-name source question without changing implementation; resumed the queued camera task rather than repeating that lookup. No blocker was present. Implemented this bounded slice; parent M6.04/M6.08 and the complete goal remain open.
- `camera_cuts.gd` represents hard cuts with ordinary Animation markers. Both pose/lens keys are required at each source frame. Continuous tracks use ordinary preceding-key easing 0 to hold the previous shot; easing 1 retains interpolation elsewhere. Names/colors/frames and changed hold keys survive sparse `camera_edits.gd` differences and reconstruction. Marker-only and hold-only mods contain no unchanged pose/lens values. Validation rejects fractional/out-of-duration markers, absent paired keys, invalid colors and invalid hold references. Matching by integer source frame avoids float serialization differences in Animation key times.
- `cinematic_camera.gd` latches history reset on crossed markers (including skipped frames), clip identity/generation changes, ownership return and stage reconfiguration. Repeated/paused samples do not repeatedly reset. `runtime.gd` consumes the request immediately before the next actual world/background render and logs `MGS_CAMERA_CUT`. Native simulation, source-frame selection, audio and callback code are unchanged.
- Verified the exact pinned Godot `c971f93e7` sources, saved read-only evidence under ignored `build/godot-sdk-m0/engine-history/`. Forward+ hardcodes FSR2 `reset_accumulation=false`; no public direct reset exists. `camera_history.gd` invokes `RenderingServer.viewport_set_fsr_sharpness` with the existing value, which reconfigures the render buffers once and destroys FSR2 history without changing AA/resolution settings. Fog custom buffers survive this engine reconfiguration, so a temporary camera Environment copy uses temporal weight 0 for the cut frame, retains history writes, then restores the authored environment after both views render. This workaround is pinned-version behavior and can allocate buffers; it is not a zero-cost engine reset and must be reverified on upgrade.
- Headless `camera_cuts_test.gd` passes continuous AnimationPlayer/runtime sampling before/at/after cuts, source-frame skipping, pause, same-clip restart, native-only clip transition, skip/end, stage unload, invalid edits and marker-only/hold-only sparse data. Extended `cinematic_preview_test.gd` passes save/export/reopen of cut names/colors/frames with changed keys, source validation and original-data stripping. Logs `cuts-test.log`, `cuts-preview-test.log`. `scene_snapshot_test`, `map_session_test`, `mod_contract_test`, `native_context_test` also pass (`cuts-<test>.log`); runtime check-only and PowerShell AST checks pass.
- GPU `camera_history_test.gd` passes on Vulkan Forward+ / RX 9070 XT. Two warm views and one fresh view demonstrate old fog history and correct clearing: normalized first-frame error versus fresh view **0.000000** with reset vs **0.410690** without. Following-frame output stays clean; FSR2 color-buffer RID changes on reset and stays stable afterward; settings/shared environment stay unchanged; world-environment fallback ownership restores correctly. Small 192x128 fixture cut interval **1.98 ms** is diagnostic only, not 1440p cadence acceptance. Logs `cuts-history-test.log/.err` (empty final stderr). ### Editable world-effect materials - M4.03d (13 October)

- Previous goal turn made verified camera/fog implementation progress. This turn adds a usable editor-to-runtime material workflow instead of more camera or elevator probes. The Inspector edits normal ShaderMaterial resources. New `effect_authoring.gd` copies the asset-free color/depth shaders and shared includes into the active workspace, creates or reopens its material and records a sparse `effects` manifest entry. The plugin lists synthetic Snow alongside original texture IDs and opens the material through the normal resource Inspector. Scene browser status now includes effect texture edits, verified cinematic inheritance and a separate global snow notice.
- `effect_materials.gd` loads and validates spatial material/depth shader pairs and the required native geometry uniforms. Runtime batch copies receive the original positions/UVs/counts, blend flags and lifetimes; the authored resource is not mutated. Appearance controls include tint/opacity, custom texture, matching alpha cutoff, roughness/metallic/specular, emissive energy and additional emission. Shared shader code keeps default sidedness and alpha coverage consistent in color/depth. Custom shader authors can edit the copied source; arbitrary custom code must preserve the documented geometry/depth contract. Multi-pass materials are rejected. Default world effects retain the existing tests' lighting, fog, native fading, ordering and expiration behavior.
- `reference_materials.gd` prepares overrides before playback; `world_effects.gd` applies them only to their effect batches and logs the winning mod. `shader_warmup.gd` includes custom effect pairs. Definition identity is `mgs/effects/snow` or the complete prepared texture ID; existing priority/ID ordering and digest-matched gameplay/cinematic inheritance apply. Snow is global wherever emitted. Per-emitter selection, arbitrary effect scenes/simulation, live reload and fully animated editor previews remain open. Existing compatibility-only effects are not suddenly owned by Godot. No native simulation, C bridge, protocol or vendor changes were made.
- ZIP exporter traverses effect material, depth shader, includes and custom textures; importer requires both manifest references. PNG/WebP dependencies load with mipmaps without relying on a local `.godot` import cache. The first raw-PNG probe demonstrated Godot cannot load these external files through ResourceLoader alone; `prepare_textures` now primes explicit texture dependencies before material loading, both in runtime and editor reopening. Initial test also found new resources lacked a persistent save path; authoring now reopens the saved resource. A test archive included auto-created directory entries; extraction/counting now distinguishes directories from resource files. Final checks pass rather than suppressing these errors.

### Optical camouflage state and material authoring - M4.04a (13 October)

- Turn classification: **verified implementation progress**, not a complete-goal or M4.04 parent acceptance. User's online map-name question was answered using https://github.com/FoxdieTeam/mgs_reversing#overlays; the existing searchable scene list already reads the pinned `stage_names.json` reference and shows classification/disc/mod status.
- Native `equip/kogaku2.c` keeps its original packet rewriting, object flags and thermal switching. Added presentation hooks at both constructors, transition updates, thermal enter/exit and both cleanup paths. `src/mgs_godot_scene.c` stores kind/amount/tint per instance, clears on reuse, and owns known cloaked KMD parts without treating their framebuffer texture pages as a fallback requirement. No gameplay/action timing or RNG change. Original NewKogaku3 saved-flag behavior was not altered.
- Bridge capability 8192 / protocol **15.11** appends twelve bytes to each instance header; current negotiated header is 48 bytes, capability mask **0x3fff**. Consumer validates kind, finite bounded amount and RGB word; native rejects invalid transition inputs. Previous layouts still decode when the capability is absent. Current native requires its matching minor. `get_instances()` adds a copied appearance dictionary for scripts without exposing mutable native state.
- `object_appearance.gd` / `camouflage.gdshader` use Godot opaque-scene refraction and the model's ordinary albedo/UVs. Restore saved surface/whole-mesh overrides when deactivated or reconfigured. Hide original packet metadata and opaque shadows while cloaked. Cache copies across unchanged samples; refresh material state if a part becomes visible, transparency changes or source stage changes. Authored materials are not mutated.
- Editor **Edit optical camouflage material** creates/reopens a copied ShaderMaterial/shader under the active mod. Registry `appearances.camouflage.material` is global and follows existing mod precedence. Normal Inspector controls adjust distortion, blur, tint and rim appearance. Export/import includes custom dependencies and rejects out-of-folder paths; shared scene-browser notice reports the edit. Per-character targeting and simulation preview are still open. The modding/protocol guides document shader inputs and limits.
- Default visual intentionally fades the whole model into refraction rather than reproducing the original screen-space vertical wipe. Refraction sees opaque Godot content, not transparent particles/other cloaked actors. Background fog is already in the sampled image; the base-color portion during transition is not separately fogged. ### Thermal model heat and editable compositor - M4.04c progress (13 October)

- Turn classification: **verified implementation progress**. - Source evidence: `goggleir.c` sets `STATE_THERMG` after three actor ticks and restores it/palette callbacks on destruction. Standard `libdg/bound.c` and fog `takabe/libdg2.c` replace CLUTs for `DG_FLAG_IRTEXTURE` (0x0200); `libdg/light.c` supplies thermal ambient (130,104,80). `bullet/jirai.c` reveals mines in thermal mode; `enemy/wall.c` and `takabe/dummy_fl.c` change model shading; `takabe/gas_efct.c` suppresses its ordinary overlay. Those simulation/visibility paths remain unchanged. Native classification is broader than characters and must not be guessed from model names.
- Protocol **15.13**, capability **32768**, full scene set **0xffff**. Thermal ownership requires vision context (and its existing dependencies). Existing schema-5 context and instance flags carry the required mode/IR classification; no unused wire fields were added. Godot disables the thermal compositor for older peers that did not negotiate ownership. `scn_mask.c` suppresses only its matching thermal mask while the Godot scene owns it. Reference rendering, Legacy and missing-capability paths retain original drawing.
- `thermal_sources.gd` mirrors IR-marked visible mesh parts into a separate heat pass, sharing Mesh/Skin/Skeleton references and following transforms. Final camera layer exclusions, hidden parts, shadow-only parts, empty original surfaces, authored removals and visibility-range settings are respected. Helper meshes cast no shadows and contribute no GI, use reserved Godot layer 18, and are excluded from exported snapshots. `visual_mod_api.gd` adds `register_thermal_source(mesh, heat)` / `unregister_thermal_source(mesh)` for custom meshes and a copied `thermal` flag in `get_instances()`. Registration validates an attached mesh and finite heat in [0,1]; authored values take precedence over native defaults. Removal/retirement restores the native default or retires the helper.
- `thermal_view.gd` is a child of the world viewport so its heat/depth texture is ready before world compositing. `depth_capture.gd` adds an optional heat-channel capture to its RG32F output (red linear depth in native millimeters, green heat). `thermal_vision_effect.gd` compares that depth with final scene depth and independently colors cold world content and hot sources. Empty background gets the cold filter without a heat capture. Main world alpha is preserved; mode switches use the existing temporal reset. The heat viewport and helper meshes are disabled/retired when thermal mode ends. No GPU readback was introduced into the runtime.
- Shared compositor hooks in `night_vision_effect.gd` support thermal bindings and projection constants without duplicating the dispatch implementation. GPU checks found PackedByteArray parameters were being mutated when projection constants were appended on subsequent frames; the render-thread copy now duplicates the packed parameters first. Final multi-frame/moving/skinned tests pass. Cold tint/gain/lift/contrast, hot tint/heat curve and scanline/grain settings are editable through **Edit thermal vision effect** in the normal Inspector.

The generated resource, thermal script and shared vision script round-trip with the manifest as exactly **four files**, without original assets. Export dependency scanning now follows and relocates literal GDScript `extends` references. The scene list reports the global thermal edit. - `tools/godot_assets/ui_formats.py`: bounded 4-bit TIM/RPK readers and static SGT vector decoding. Invalid extents, offsets, unsupported/animated layouts and primitive types fail explicitly. Quad winding, native reverse ordering-table draw order, RGB and semi-transparency mode are retained in semantic JSON. No original vectors or pixel data are embedded in source.
- `ui_fonts.py` records the recovered `menu/radio.c` glyph metrics: case folding, narrow I, punctuation, eight-pixel line advance, 6x5 small glyphs and 8x6 large glyphs. `ui_assets.py` prepares only init 8504/8505/8506 and 84db/84dc/84dd plus `c70e.r` (num TIM) and image 45/palette 44 of `5d43.r` (item RPK). The num palette's zero entry is cleared exactly as `menu_number_init` does. Other SGTs/fonts are not claimed supported.
- Normal asset preparation now includes these eight resources. `--ui-only` / PowerShell `-UIOnly` extends an existing matching cache without rebuilding geometry or cinematics. Both real-disc caches were updated in `.local` only; receipts/source files and the user's mod ZIP were not changed.
- Python/Godot prepared provenance recognizes `ui_layout` and `ui_font`; atlas PNGs are hashed dependencies. The full catalogs each verify **22,735 assets / 43,449 files / zero unverified assets** (`ui-provenance-disc1.log`, `ui-provenance-disc2.log`). Root aggregation remains compatible.
- `original_ui_assets.gd` creates shared FontFile resources for ordinary Labels, checks source/dependencies and glyph bounds, supports font size and color overrides, and disables system-font fallback. Fonts carry `mgs_retail_derived`, so the existing exporter rejects accidentally saved original bitmap data. ### 2026-10-13 — M5.05a.1 shared settings registry and persistence bridge

The preceding turn was **progress**: title/ZIP implementation and evidence changed
the authoritative worktree. This continuation implements the first shared settings
backend. M5.05a remains **in progress** because actual live display application,
confirmation/revert and applied GPU/native state are not implemented yet.

Implemented:

- `launcher/DisplaySettings.cs` owns resolution choices, type checks and the
  existing native-compatible dimension/aspect bounds. `GameLaunch` and the launcher
  use it. `SettingsRegistry.cs` exposes typed definitions, requested launch values,
  saved values, revision and explicit restart requirements. Read/validate/save
  merge only known display fields into the existing XML profile; validation and
  rejected/stale requests do not write it. Other settings and mod selections survive.
- `SettingsBridge.cs` serves bounded, session-authenticated JSON on an ephemeral
  loopback socket. The launcher owns its synchronous worker thread; shutdown closes
  the listener/client before joining. `GodotSession` guarantees renderer cleanup
  even if service disposal reports a failure. Launcher exit/restart refreshes saved
  display fields and rebuilds native arguments without overwriting other active options.
- `SettingsHost.cs` / `settings-host.exe` provide the same backend for
  `tools/run-godot.ps1`, including editor native-test sessions. Their runtime profile
  uses `settings.xml`; absent CLI display parameters use saved values, explicit
  parameters override this launch only. `-Fullscreen` now reaches the Godot window.
  The launcher build includes the helper. These are local presentation settings,
  not a new gameplay/save format or a second settings store.
- `settings_client.gd` provides asynchronous bounded requests, deadlines, schema/
  identity/value validation, revision conflicts, error signals and retained state.
  `visual_mod_api.gd.get_settings_provider()` exposes it to mod scripts. Missing
  host configuration is explicit. Initial read occurs only after renderer readiness:
  the first live boot found that connecting before shader warmup could time out
  while the main thread was unable to send. The corrected boot proves that fix.
- [Settings contract](SETTINGS_CONTRACT.md) documents the public API, local wire,
  persistence, override semantics and limits. Native protocol stays **15.23** and
  vendor stack stays **117**; no native source/vendor changes in this slice.

### 2026-10-13 — M5.05a.2 live display transactions

The preceding Codec-only turn rechecked an already validated correction; it did
not advance the SDK milestone. This continuation is **implementation and
verification progress**. The interrupted display work is now integrated, built
and tested. The full goal remains active and unblocked.

Implemented:

- Protocol **15.24**, operation **26 DISPLAY**, capability **33554432**, complete
  mask **0x3ffffff**. `MgsGodotDisplayTick` runs before native actor updates,
  publishes actual resolution/aspect with session identity and acknowledges each
  strictly increasing request before releasing the boundary. Native changes
  presentation/culling together; wide-to-original resets the ratio and override.
  Codec/movie/menu original-canvas behavior survives changes. Fullscreen is
  owned by the real Godot window, not the native transport window.
- `display_settings.gd` validates full candidates based on actual applied values
  through the existing host. It coordinates GPU prepare/native acknowledgement/
  GPU commit, a wall-clock **15-second** confirmation, cancellation and rollback.
  Persistence happens only on explicit confirmation. A lost save response is
  reconciled with a read; unreadable persistence remains an explicit error, not
  a hidden success or an automatic overwrite of newer settings.
- `display_surface.gd` operates the Godot render thread, textures, world/metadata
  extents, temporal reset and window mode/size. Previous GPU buffers remain
  allocated during a preview. Restoration reuses them and refreshes their VRAM
  from the current native texture, preserving uploads made during the preview.
  Window changes are checked after application. Fullscreen uses desktop size;
  the chosen resolution controls internal scale as before.
- `settings_client.gd.display` exposes the transaction API to ordinary mod
  scripts. `get_snapshot()` and `settings_updated` report actual applied state,
  phase/error/countdown and `live_confirm` definitions when the host and native
  capability are present. The raw storage snapshot remains distinct.
- A real world resize exposed automatically invalidated cached uniform sets in
  `world_blit.gd`. The compositor now checks RenderingDevice validity before
  freeing/reusing them and rebuilds bindings for resized viewport textures.
- Patch **118**, `patches/mgs-godot-display.patch`, pins only the native main-loop
  integration. `display-patches.log`: all 118 patches match. Tests are registered
  in CMake and the Godot runner; the opt-in dock fixture contains only source.

### 2026-10-13 — M5.06b editable display panel and input capture

Previous Codec-only turn: **no progress** toward the SDK goal; it restated existing
verification without changing the next action. This turn implements and verifies
M5.06b. The full goal remains active, with no external blocker.

Implemented `display_menu_scene.gd`, `display_menu_ui.gd`, `display_menu.gd`:
normal Controls/Theme, DISPLAY entry and F10/R3, resolution/aspect/fullscreen,
Apply/Keep/Revert, countdown, safe focus and busy/error feedback. Uses the shared
live provider, without another store. Host snapshot updates preserve in-progress
edits until applied state changes. Back during preview restores first and keeps
input ownership until the transaction settles. Simulation explicitly continues.

`ui.display_settings.scene` / **Edit Display Settings UI** creates an editable
scene; original font binding, editor preview/save cleanup, custom placement and
ZIP reopening pass. Export contains exactly manifest, TSCN and UI script. Fonts
are not exported. Original Options gameplay actions remain unchanged.

Protocol **15.25**, capability **67108864**, full mask **0x7ffffff**: reply bit 30
captures host input. Godot suppresses keyboard forwarding; `input_capture.c`
neutralizes digital buttons and analog axes on both SDL logical ports before
recording/replay. Connection/type identity is preserved. Ports and forwarded
keys require release before control returns. Authoritative replay remains so;
pause/audio/timing are unchanged. Psyz patch **29** pins the SDL integration;
MGS stack remains **118**.

### 2026-10-13 — M5.11b direct Options actions and native menu Controls

Classification: **progress**. This slice implements the original Options actions;
it does not close M5 or change the full delivery boundary.

Implementation:

- `mgs_options_actions.h/.c` owns the sequenced semantic-revision queue; native
  `opt.c` consumes explicit select/set/activate/back actions using existing menu
  helpers and `SetGameOptionFlag`. It preserves unrelated configuration bits,
  staged values until apply/exit, audio mode, vibration cleanup and menu sounds.
  Original persistence remains through native `GM_Configuration`/GCL save state,
  not a new renderer-owned settings file. Source ownership: `linkvar.h` slot 2
  and `libgcl/variable.c` existing initialization/save/load paths.
- Options op 23 uses former reserved header word 12 for semantic revision when
  action capability is negotiated; op 28 exchanges 32-byte action/ack payloads.
  Session, epoch, generation/revision and action availability are checked;
  invalid/stale/busy user requests are recoverable, malformed sequences fatal.
- Ordinary Buttons sit under the named original artwork nodes. Inspector fields
  specify native action/target/value; moving the artwork moves its interaction
  region. Default root handles keyboard/controller navigation, focused-button
  confirm, mouse, native capture and Display-panel suspension. Errors appear in
  the editable local-font `ActionError` Label. Original asset references detach
  before packing/export and bind on reopening.
- Shared provider mechanics live in `native_menu_actions.gd`; Save actions only
  retain their own validation/encoding. Documentation and `test-godot.ps1` updated.
